ut her worst journeys. He emerged laughing. The ring remained in his pocket. His planned proposal had not perished, merely changed shape. A quiet bench would do better than a wheel that made him dizzy.","Mara hurriedly put a chair beside the path for a shaking passenger. A genius was not required to notice a tired person. The innocent guests had been frightened, and no victim owed the crowd a cheerful performance. One thanked her; another simply sat. She let both responses be enough.","The operator closed the ride for a full inspection. The evening's fireworks would continue. Cassian could have complained about their unused reservation; instead he thanked the staff. The main point was that people had come down safely. Their ticket would be refunded. He would survive the loss of one panoramic photograph.","The refund clerk promised to reinforce the old reservation record with a clear note. The keyword was CREDIT. He had been working mightily to answer everyone, and his formerly monotonous job had become complicated. Mara would spare no effort to make it simpler: she and Cassian could wait ten minutes or so while a family caught its last bus.","Mara's cousin returned with cold drinks. Her impulsive decision to book the wheel had been kindly meant. There was no reason to punish herself for an equipment problem. The park's reigning manager promised to review the balloon rigging. A profit-oriented business had reasons to keep people safe; its commitments would be tested in the repairs it made after visitors left.","The music resumed. A guitarist played the song instrumentally one last time, then a singer joined him. Cassian asked Mara whether they might indulge themselves in a dance. She accepted in return for his surrender of the paper crown. He pretended to consider a better bargain. She took it off him anyway.","The motion was simple. Their steps did not follow a linear path around the pavilion; Mara kept turning towards the fireworks' first light. Cassian followed her lead. He had no desire to take the evening's decisions away from her. The supposed power of his title had been more useful for carrying bags than commanding the moon.","To the naked eye, the first white flash seemed almost still. Then its stars fell. Cassian was inclined to seek to do nothing except hold her closer. It was an optimistic plan and a welcome one. Mara decided that was worth more than another panoramic ride or a flawless evening's schedule.","A fireworks rehearsal began. The sound crew unplugged a spare amplifier before the first burst. The park had lowered the volume in response to neighbours. A special-interest group could ask for a change without becoming the enemy of a celebration. The main point was to agree on something concrete, and the quieter test had done that.","The labour rights representative had also accepted a meeting. Nobody wanted to use volunteers instrumentally, then thank them instead of letting them rest. A would-be champion of community could learn from the people doing the work. The park's good reputation would perish if it treated those people as scenery. Mara hoped the next season would give them a better answer.","Her cousin looked at her. “Must every outing turn into a lesson?”","“Only if you keep bringing me to places with broken balloons.”","Cassian laughed. “I intend to take a page from this evening and stop booking heights.”","“To put it another way, you have lost an argument with a wheel.”","“An expensive one.”","They walked to the lake. The first real firework opened above the water, white fading into gold. Mara leaned into Cassian's side. It was beautiful enough to invert her sense of scale: the huge black sky seemed near, the distant music tiny and warm. He kissed her temple, and she decided the ground had advantages of its own.","His phone lit up. A message from her cousin showed a photograph of the quiet-bench proposal. There had been a yes. Cassian smiled and put the phone away before any business message could follow. Tonight he had no further need to seek an outcome.","Mara gave him back the paper crown. “You may keep that.”","He bowed extravagantly. A royal privilege at last."],"questions":[],"wordCount":722,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0240\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：公爵输给了一只摩天轮</div><h1>The Duke Snowstorm","volume":"婚后外传：星星来得太晚了","anchor":"chapter-0241","paragraphs":["Cassian had booked an iconic mountain lodge because Mara wanted to see stars. The stars had been replaced by a snowstorm. He looked mildly depressed by the substitution. She promised not to hold him responsible for atmospheric conditions, provided he produced supper.","Their luggage had arrived from a Hilton hotel in the valley, along with an orchestral ensemble whose concert had been cancelled. Industrial action had closed the normal lift connection. The musicians respected the dispute, but nobody had expected to spend the night between a weather laboratory and a dining room in ruins. The old wing was permanently closed after an earlier roof failure; the newer rooms were warm and safe.","They had spent three nights in a row travelling. The ensemble's bond had survived fatigue, delayed luggage and an iconic hotel carpet that shed fluff. Their Hilton bill included complimentary breakfast; the lodge offered something less formal. That said, nobody complained about everyday food. The byproduct of a difficult journey was an appetite that made plain soup fantastic.","The lodge's diligent keeper, Anya, arranged a joint meal. She had finite supplies and enough experience to optimize them. A ray of sunshine might have improved her mood, but hot soup would do. She gave Cassian a knife and asked him to chop vegetables. His qualified smile suggested he had intended to invest in dinner rather than produce it.","Mara, for her part, had no cooking advantage over the musicians. Anya called it a level playing field and handed everyone a board. Even better, the task gave them fresh impetus to talk. A visitor could displace a little anxiety by cutting carrots. Cassian's failure to produce equal pieces became the evening's least dangerous attraction.","A loose bolt had let the door frame flex. Its replacement gave the technician more leverage. The fix would insulate the room without trapping an unsafe heater inside it. The broken hinge, once a benign inconvenience, could have become worse. They would not ignore it anymore. Transparency about these small repairs seemed vastly more reassuring than a promise of perfection.","Cassian habitually reached for a phone when a problem appeared. Tonight he could not buy a breeze. Social media had no current information more useful than the keeper's call to the guide. He put it away. To say the least, the change in method was educational. Mara liked his willingness to go beyond the familiar response.","Mara joined two students at the lab window. One hoped to major in meteorology. The other studied thermal imaging. A recorded lecture compared hurricane and typhoon tracks; a wall display showed snow and hail over different geographic regions. The students' fortnightly project tracked cloud patterns, not every phenomenon in the atmosphere. Their educated guesses could improve a local forecast without promising certainty.","A large-scale map showed the expected trajectory of the snow. Beyond the window, fog had hidden the lower road. Lightning flashed once. The students did not speculate about an exact opening time; they sought to understand the available observations. Their teacher asked them to seek to identify useful changes, then take action only within the safety plan.","The wind began to wreak trouble on the outer door. A loose strip of flashing made a metallic puff with every gust. The keeper prohibited guests from using the exposed terrace. A whirlwind of loose snow crossed it. The lodge was not literally inside a tornado, Anya told a worried violinist. The storm was dangerous enough without reimagining it.","The ridge looked like a monumental wall of haze. Anya would not exert pressure on the driver to attempt it. His family already worried about the conditions. A small local scheme helped disadvantaged students visit the lab, but it was unfunded this month. She hoped to reimagine the programme when the route reopened. Its resources were finite, its ambitions doubtless less so.","A fume from the fireplace made Mara look up. The keeper had already sent for maintenance. A glowing ember had caught a paper edge, and a sprinkle of sparks fell onto the hearth. Nobody would let a picturesque fire kill off their good sense. The technician shut it down safely. The room's warmth would have to come from the ordinary heaters.","The lodge's electricity came through the regional grid, whose mix included nuclear power. Tonight a backup system supported essential equipment. The technician checked voltage before using a spare unit. Its thermal label had faded, though the case was intact. Mara liked watching careful work. Anya had been asked to optimize costs for years; safe equipment was not the place to save pennies.","The lodge's old Napoleonic portrait hung above a barometer in a sealed case. A former owner had collected both. Neither would enlighten them about tomorrow's weather, but the portrait's severe face seemed to prohibit complaints. Cassian gave it a glance while stirring soup. “I am unqualified to satisfy that gentleman.”","The aspiring meteorologist laughed. She had planned to major in science rather than kitchen logistics, but could help lay tables. Her classmate showed Mara a portable x-ray image of the barometer's repaired support. It revealed the shape of a concealed fastener, not the age of the whole instrument. They had no need to speculate beyond it.","The barometer's sealed glass gave a clear view of its scale. An ultraviolet lamp had once helped a conservator examine its old finish. The keeper's notes covered a spectrum of prior repairs. Mara read them appreciatively. The portrait's priestly solemnity could not enlighten her about the instrument, but the records could.","A sudden crash sounded outside. A branch, sheared by the wind, struck the shed. In the torrent of streaming snow beyond it, a small light moved. Someone was on the path. Mara reached for her coat before Cassian could finish saying her name."],"questions":[],"wordCount":963,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0241\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div Anya stopped Mara at the door. The route would take a trained guide, a rope team and proper equipment. Cassian had no desire to argue. He pulled Mara's scarf into place and helped carry supplies to the safe entrance.","The old wing remained permanently closed. Its roof was in ruins, and a shortcut through it was prohibited. The diligent guide would not displace one danger with another. Its walls could not insulate a trapped person from falling debris. The keeper shut the connecting door and directed the team towards the proper exit.","Lightning flickered beyond the ridge. The snowstorm had obscured every familiar line. Hail rattled against the lower windows, and a dense fog lay beneath the terrace. The laboratory's thermal camera helped the guide locate a warm figure by the shed. It did not tell him the condition of the path. His geographic knowledge would matter as much as the screen.","The student who hoped to major in meteorology watched the guide take action. Their educated guesses about the evening had not predicted this exact problem. A model's finite resolution left small local details uncertain. She would not denounce forecasting because it could not see every branch. Nor would she let an impressive image replace a person who knew the ground.","The current storm gave the fortnightly project fresh impetus. The student would invest time in its local details, not just broad maps. She could seek to learn from people who worked outside. The lab's x-ray archive was irrelevant to locating warmth. The difference in purpose would enlighten her more than displaying every instrument at once.","Inside, the orchestral players waited. One habitually pessimistic cellist looked depressed. Another said their cancelled concert now seemed benign compared with losing a friend. The ensemble's bond was unmistakable. Mara gave them something useful to do: clear the entrance, warm blankets and make room for the guide's return. They responded with a diligence more convincing than any grand speech.","A whirlwind of activity filled the kitchen. Streaming water darkened the guide's spare coat. There was no sunshine to warm it, only the radiator and patience. The teacher had to prohibit a visitor from blocking the heater. A casual lack of attention could create trouble even when everyone meant well. Doubtless they had all learned that tonight.","A violin lay on the table, its varnish glowing beneath the lamp. A sprinkle of damp snow had reached the lid. Mara dried the outside without opening the cold instrument immediately. Anya provided a stable room. The case could insulate it through the change, while a specialised repairer would assess any damage later. The player was an educated musician, not a varnish specialist.","Industrial action had left the iconic lodge's usual transport inaccessible. Anya would not speculate about an exception. That said, she asked the rescue coordinator about tomorrow's options. The reply gave her a little leverage for planning. A byproduct of waiting was the discovery that she could not organize every minute herself. Even better, nobody expected her to.","The portrait's priestly face looked down on complimentary tea. The guide would literally bring a person back through that doorway. The thought gave the room a shared gravity. No one wanted another thrill anymore. Cassian helped move the chairs, leaving a clear route from the entrance to the warmest part of the room.","The lodge's backup power remained limited. Its nuclear power supply in the wider grid could not prevent a local cable fault. The voltage warning flashed once. Maintenance disconnected a damaged appliance. A small puff escaped its casing, followed by an acrid fume. Nobody rejoiced at the malfunction, but the protective system had worked. The room remained lit.","One musician tried to go on social media for news. A fantastic number of rumours appeared, none more useful than the radio. He looked mildly ashamed. 'To say the least, I have selected the wrong source.' Anya nodded and gave him cups. He could go beyond speculation by helping the people already there.","On the laboratory's display, a ray crossed a spectrum chart. The student closed the recorded hurricane lesson and the typhoon map to free screen space. The guide needed the current thermal image. Hail, fog and chop on the distant lake were all real, but none was a reason to flood the display with every available number. The group had to optimize attention as well as power.","Mara could see the branch's trajectory against the shed roof. A shear line marked its break. Three icicles hung in a row beneath it. The weather might wreak damage without caring whether anyone had made reasonable plans. Still, the guide could work within that uncertainty. He did not need a promise of absolute safety to make a useful decision.","The weight of snow could exert pressure on the broken frame. The guide's plan accounted for it. A large-scale rescue fantasy would not improve the task. Anya would not reimagine herself as a mountain expert merely because she was afraid. Her job was to keep the safe entrance clear. She did it with a severity worthy of the Napoleonic portrait.","Cassian stood beside her, the keeper's radio in his hand. The Napoleonic portrait watched over them with the same severe face. “Still unqualified to command weather,” he murmured.","Anya had received a call from the Hilton about the stranded guests. Its manager offered rooms when the road opened. They could rejoice in that prospect later. Her own fortune had not made the storm obey her. In its aftermath she would have repairs, paperwork and a new respect for a simple hot meal. The violinist's life mattered more than any bill.","“Excellent. Leave it to people who know it.”","He kissed her brow, then turned as the radio crackled. The guide had reached the violinist. She was cold and shaken, her case wedged behind the damaged shed. midnight. The violinist walked through the door between two guides, pale and angry at herself. Her colleagues wrapped her in blankets and then began talking all at once. Anya stopped the flood with hot tea. The next decision could wait until the rescued woman could feel her hands.","Mara sat with Cassian beneath the iconic lodge's enormous window. The snowstorm had weakened. A single ray of morning sunshine was still hours away, but the wind's force was easing. His careful plan had collapsed into a night of vegetables, blankets and waiting. She leaned against him and said it had still been a fantastic evening to spend together.","He looked at her. “To say the least, your standards are flexible.”","“My standards concern you. The weather may apply separately.”","The orchestral group would remain until the road reopened. Industrial action still affected the lift; an independent transport service could take them down when conditions allowed. Nobody would exert pressure on striking staff to make an unsafe exception. Their rescued friend had already created enough work for the mountain team.","The aftermath would give the keeper enough work for weeks. A torrent of melting snow could displace damaged branches, so the path stayed closed. A mild breeze now moved the haze along the ridge. The geographic map's warning zones still applied. Mara would not infer safety merely because the sky looked kinder.","At breakfast, the aspiring student said she still wanted to major in meteorology. The night had been enlightening rather than discouraging. Anya promised to show her the fortnightly local records. She had invested years in the observations. The research was modest, mostly unfunded and useful to everyday decisions. Its significance did not require a monumental headline.","Anya doubted whether the night had killed anyone's affection for the mountain. The weather might kill off a few romantic illusions, but she would take that trade. Her warmth gave the student fresh impetus. The proposed research had no grand budget; it could still seek to answer a useful local question. Even better, it could start with observations already kept.","Streaming meltwater crossed the terrace that had stood in ruins for months. The keeper would take action when engineers could examine it. She intended to prohibit casual access permanently. A visitor's desire for a photograph could not literally insulate them from a fall. The closed gate remained firmly shut.","The lab retained its hurricane and typhoon maps. A snow display beside them showed hail under a microscope. A teacher could go beyond spectacular imagery by asking what each scale left out. Today's educated guesses concerned the road opening, and the guide would check it before anyone moved. Human judgment and measurement remained a joint effort.","The thaw would doubtless bring another set of small problems. Cassian watched water gather beneath the eaves, mildly fascinated by its persistence. His fortune could not buy a faster sunrise. It could help the disadvantaged students visit when the road was safe. Anya accepted that offer, provided the project could go on without publicity conditions.","Cassian had become the diligent keeper of the breakfast toast. He had to chop more vegetables for soup, a bond Anya seemed unwilling to release. Mara watched a sprinkle of flour land on his sleeve. The bread's crust was glowing. A small puff of steam rose when he cut it. The pleasant smell replaced the previous night's fume, and he looked relieved.","At dinner, the musicians wanted to rejoice quietly. The student offered to enlighten Cassian about a cloud chart. He said the thrust of its argument was that mountains were inconvenient. She laughed and corrected him. He accepted the explanation with such visible relief that Mara knew he had been waiting to hear an ordinary voice again.","The saved violin was not permanently damaged, though its case needed drying and repair. Its x-ray image, taken before the tour for an insurance record, had not helped the rescue. The current thermal camera had. Mara liked the plain distinction. Neither technology was magic; each could be useful to a particular question.","The technician restored electricity to the normal circuits. Nuclear power remained one distant part of the grid, not an answer to a snapped local line. The backup unit had finite capacity, hence the restrictions. Cassian accepted the explanation without pretending to understand every component. He was not too proud to be unqualified.","The cold air had briefly depressed the backup unit's performance. Maintenance would examine it properly. Cassian's fortune could pay for another unit, but he would not speculate about which one. The byproduct of his helpless night was a little humility. He habitually bought solutions; today he would invest in advice before choosing equipment.","By afternoon, snow began to fall from the eaves. The broken branch's trajectory was visible against fresh drifts. Shear from the wind had left a sharp pale edge. A stream of meltwater ran beneath it; above, a narrow ray crossed the fog. The sunshine felt earned even though nobody had persuaded it to appear.","The view could make a person forget how hard a storm could wreak damage. A whirlwind of light snow crossed the inaccessible path below the ruined wing. Lightning scars marked an older tree farther down. The restoration scheme would have to reimagine the damaged terrace, not simply rebuild the same weak edge. A large-scale redesign could wait for engineers.","Anya asked whether they would return. The Hilton in the valley would be more convenient. Cassian looked at Mara before answering. She was smiling at the musicians tuning up beside the sealed Napoleonic portrait. Their first notes were tentative, then rich. He decided convenience might be overrated.","That night, the stars arrived. Mara and Cassian went only as far as the safe window. She stood in front of him, his arms warm around her waist, and counted three bright points in a row before he kissed the back of her neck. She lost the count willingly. There would be other stars."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1000,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0243\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：星星来得太晚了</div><h1>Stars Have dignity. The pumpkin had been grown for the harvest contest and weighed more than Mara had expected. The rules required him to move it around a circle without tipping it out. She had volunteered as his navigator. Her advice so far consisted mainly of laughter.","The garden stood beside a national park. Peta had arranged the visit to show first-time growers practical work in action. A pine sheltered the entrance, its bark rough beneath a display of old pruning tools. Nearby, garlic and broccoli were being packed for the kitchen. A weed threatened to swallow the sign for the seed exchange. Mara pulled it out, pleased to contribute something less complicated than wheelbarrow advice.","Milo, a millennial gardener with a literary ambition, had written an account of the community plot. It contained a chapter on social mobility and another on the embarrassment of killing every sprout in one's first tray. He hoped the combination was enlightening. His publisher thought it existential. His grandmother thought both words far too long for a book about cabbage.","The grandmother's soup contained wheat, garlic and a small cultivated mushroom. A single peanut had been kept away from a child with an allergy. The cook checked the label before serving. Her orderly habits were more useful than the ornate sign above her table. She wanted guests to enjoy food, not prove bravery by eating an unknown ingredient.","The plot had begun after a neighbour lost her job. Ever since, its volunteers had tried to take advantage of spare land without idealizing hard work. Most of the food was largely ordinary: wheat, cherry tomatoes, grass paths and a bush of late flowers. They knew of no godlike grower who could eliminate weather. More often than not, success meant returning to water a tray one had forgotten yesterday.","Cassian wanted to idealize the little garden until a wretched tomato hit his shoe. Its stem had broken. The fruit seemed likely to stain the leather, and Mara could not rein in her delight. She did not know of an inborn gift for making a duke look foolish. Hers had developed through practice. To the extent that this was a talent, he had cultivated it himself.","Peta asked Mara to help with a flower diagram. The ovary would develop into fruit after fertilization, she explained, while the seed contained an embryo. The children could comprehend the model before looking at real flowers. A tiny bulb sat beside a larger root, and a pressed blossom showed the shape of an earlier bud. The comparison was evidence-based enough for their question, without pretending every plant behaved identically.","Mara found a pine needle on her sleeve and a clove in the soup. The cook had added far too much pepper, so she reached for a cucumber slice to cool her mouth. Cassian offered a nut biscuit instead. She declined with a look that made him reconsider the recipe. Even a sincere gift could be maladapted to its moment.","An everlasting flower had been dried for a child's craft project. The girl wanted to poke it into the grass beside a model cereal field. Her teacher asked her to make a hole first. Mara admired this small intervention. Even imaginary farming benefited from a pause before force.","A failed seed tray helped spell out the importance of conditions. A cactus could be maladapted to a wet corner without being a bad plant. Unrecognized stress could kill it in short order. The lesson was ironic beneath an enormous poster promising effortless growth. Peta refused to let the claim go by without correction. Her verbal precision pleased Mara.","The old greenhouse had an ornate door and an ineffective drain. Water threatened to permeate the bench whenever rain came. A weld on its frame needed inspection. The gardener would get cracking on a repair after the contest. He wanted to insure the building, but the first estimate had been inflationary enough to make his grandmother swear. No policy could substitute for maintenance.","The gardener would not cheat by hiding a failed test. Thrift mattered, but an unsound frame could not be wished sound. Unhealthy seedlings had already been moved away from it. Prior to reopening, he would check the roof. He would specialize in plants, he said, and leave the metal to a welder. Mara understood that boundary. Besides, there was plenty of ordinary work waiting for everyone.","The seed bank gave away half a million seeds each spring. Its volunteers worked efficiently, though first-time visitors often misunderstood the number. Samples were small and plentiful; they were not half a million rare species. Peta's talk on the fossil record made a similar distinction between a specimen and the world it might help describe. Mara liked the care she took.","A stall collected for a rape crisis service. Its volunteers offered quiet support and information, without inviting public stories from anyone who did not want to share. The garden's sign-up welcomed them alongside the food bank. A person could need several forms of help, and neither should hinder the other.","Milo's grandmother began to orchestrate supper. She would dilute the garlic dressing, remold the soft cheese before plating and dispose of any cracked jars. A young helper wanted to disassemble the faulty tap first. The inspector visiting from the Food Standards Authority asked them to finish one task at a time. The body called itself an authority, but its inspector's most useful advice concerned a clean spoon.","Cassian's pumpkin shifted. Mara made a grab for the handle. The barrow tipped downward, stopped, then rocked back. He looked at her. “Are you prone to rescuing only the attractive participant?”","“Certainly. You should be grateful it is not a beauty contest.”"],"questions":[],"wordCount":959,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0244\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div
